# Advent handbook (Roman Rite, United States): source audit

Collated on 2026-11-26 (UTC) by the collation lane from the writer's ledger (`.scratch/advent-pc/writer/LEDGER.md`), the ledgers of Appendix A and Appendix B, the fix-pass dispositions (`review/FIXES.md`), the evidence pack and the five lane dossiers, the sources lane's new records and those of its second research pass, and the library records under `src/sources/`, read against the final text of the sections. One row per claim the book makes: every quotation, matched by script word for word to the record named (the passage `text` or the tracked verse text that holds it), and every statement of fact, counsel and scope around the quotations, checked against the record itself. About This Book (`95-scope.tex`) is audited like the rest. Rows are ordered by section file and line; a key begins `c` and the file's number, and a quotation's key carries `q` before its line. The bound records, their states and fingerprints are in `source-bindings.toml` beside this file.

Evidence states:

- **verified source text**: the record named is verified, and the wording or fact stands in its checked transcription (or, for page-image passages, in the record's verbatim context).
- **checked quotation or paraphrase**: the wording was checked against an exact witness short of a verified record: a tracked Scripture verse text, an inspected web text, or a page image read for a record not verified.
- **source-grounded synthesis**: a statement inferred, combined or computed from the named records.
- **editorial or AI proposal**: the book's own rendering, arrangement, counsel or scope statement. Where it renders a Latin text, the record named holds that Latin.
- **unverified lead**: a statement resting on a reading not registered as a library record. A claim of the book may not rest on a lead. No row of the matrix is in this state: the 29 statements first rowed as leads each found a witness in the second research pass, recorded at the end.

Standing qualifications that apply to every row that cites them:

- **Scripture.** English verses are the Douay-Rheims (Challoner) in the tracked Project Gutenberg 1581 verse text, Latin the Clementine Vulgate in the tracked eBible latVUC verse text, both read in the tracked file (inspected, not verified). Quotations are cited by the Douay's own book names and its chapter and verse (Vulgate numbering: Isaias, Sophonias, Micheas, Malachias, 1 and 2 Kings for 1 and 2 Samuel, Ecclesiasticus for Sirach, the psalms in the Greek numbering); the verse loci in the bindings use that numbering. The Douay's emphatic capitals are set in ordinary capitals in the book (Numbers 24:17, "A star shall rise", at 32:58 and 90:229; the record prints "A STAR SHALL RISE"), as About This Book states; those two rows are otherwise exact.
- **The U.S. Lectionary.** The *Lectionary for Mass for Use in the Dioceses of the United States*, second typical edition, is cited, never printed. Its appointments, Lectionary numbers and citations (in its own New American Bible chapter and verse and Hebrew psalm numbering) rest on the USCCB daily-readings pages registered as citation-only passages (headings and citations, retrieved 2026-11-26; the pages' wording of the readings is not held), and its edition on the copyright statement printed below those pages (Confraternity of Christian Doctrine, 2001, 1998, 1997, 1986, 1970; inspected). The Latin *Ordo lectionum Missae*, editio typica altera (1981), controls the Roman appointments, the 17–24 December rubric and the Alleluia verses; where the two differ, the book follows the U.S. pages and the row says so. No printed U.S. Lectionary volume was read.
- **The Missal of 2002.** The *Missale Romanum*, editio typica tertia (2002), was read but is not printed: its Collects, Entrance Antiphons, Prefaces, rubrics and Order of Mass are described in the book's own words and cited by place (rows `editorial or AI proposal` or `checked` against the inspected Latin record). The 2008 emended reprint rests on the Notitiae notice. The U.S. English of 2011 is never quoted; its edition rests on the publisher's title page and the USCCB release of 20 August 2010. The Advent IV Entrance Antiphon is printed only in the 1922 Tours Missal's Latin and Lasance's English, and the Advent IV Collect only as the Angelus prayer of the Raccolta of 1910.
- **The Liturgy of the Hours.** No printed Office book of the present rite was read. Its structure (Lauds as Morning Prayer, the Office of Readings in place of Matins, the Gospel canticles of Lauds, Vespers and Compline, and the commemoration of saints from 17 to 24 December) rests on *Sacrosanctum Concilium* n. 89, *Laudis canticum* n. 2 and the General Instruction (*Institutio generalis de Liturgia Horarum*) in the English of 1974 for Australia, England and Wales, and Ireland, the translation of the British and Irish Divine Office, not the one printed in the U.S. book. That English is restricted and is paraphrased with its numbers, never quoted; no public Latin text of the Instruction was found. Its appointments (the Office of Readings' second readings, the Te Deum, the antiphons at Evening Prayer) rest on the AELF daily Office service (French, Roman zone, as retrieved) and on Felix Just's table of the non-biblical second readings; the second readings are quoted only in older public-domain English, the Office's own English never. Where the present Office keeps a text of the older Office, its Latin is the Ratisbon Breviary of 1888 and its English Bute's (1908), which prints the Office as it stood before 1960.
- **Catena Aurea.** Its excerpts join or condense passages; the book sets each saying under the Father to whom the Catena gives it and credits it "as the Catena Aurea gives" him. The Catena on Mark puts Bede's sentence on the doorkeeper under Gregory's name; the book does not quote it. The Catena's "Cyril" on Luke 2:7 is quoted only as the Catena gives it.
- **Holy See texts of 1969–1975.** The Universal Norms on the Liturgical Year and the commentary published with the 1969 calendar, *Sacrosanctum Concilium*, *Mysterii Paschalis*, the constitution *Missale Romanum*, *Laudis canticum*, the General Instruction of the Liturgy of the Hours (in the English of 1974), *Marialis cultus*, the General Instruction of the Roman Missal and the Lectionary's introduction are paraphrased with their numbers, never quoted: the rows rest on the locator passages of the Latin (and, where held, the official English) and are `verified` or `checked` for the fact paraphrased, not for any wording. The same holds for the *Directory on Popular Piety* (2001), *Admirabile signum* (2019), the Code of 1983 in English, and the U.S. and Dicastery documents on penance and holy days.
- **The book's own translations.** Short renderings after a Latin or Nahuatl quotation in the running text are the book's own English (About This Book lists them). Their rows are classed `editorial or AI proposal`, and the record named holds the original: the older Roman prayers of the Gelasian Sacramentary (the postcommunion *Da, quaesumus* behind the First Sunday's Collect, *Excita*, and the prayers behind the Collects of 18, 21 and 24 December) and the Veronese prefaces (the Ascension preface behind Advent Preface I; the December preface of the *commercium*); the calendar of 354 (25 December); the Martyrology of 1902 on Nicholas, Ambrose, Damasus and Lucy; Our Lady's words in the Nahuatl *Huei tlamahuiçoltica* (1649), fol. 5r; Augustine, Sermons 185.1 and 293.3; Gregory, Hom. 8.1; Honorius, *Sacramentarium* XCI; Beleth, *Summa* LXIV; the acts of the Sixth Provincial Council of Baltimore (1846); and short glosses after the Latin of the Breviary of 1888 (*Ite obviam ei*, *Alma Redemptoris Mater*, *O Radix*), the Missal of 1922 (*Gaudete*), the Vulgate (*oriens ex alto*, Luke 1:78) and Bernard (*Surge, curre, aperi*). Passages known only in Latin are given in their sense, without quotation marks (Jerome on Isaiah 11; Gregory, Hom. 13.3 and 29.10; Durandus, *Rationale* VI.2); their rows are editorial in the same way. The English after the Latin of Gregory of Tours at 14:27 is Dalton's word for word (verified), not the book's own.
- **Page-image and web passages.** The 1966 *Pastoral Statement*, the U.S. holy-days decree, the Dicastery note and the USCCB newsletters are registered as web or PDF passages whose wording stands in the record's context and notes, not in a checked transcription artifact.
- **Display capitals.** Appendix B sets the display capitals of its sources in the ordinary way and writes V. and R. for the versicle signs (the Angelus, the *Aspiciens*). Its eight case-only differences from the records, Bute's English of the seven great O antiphons (92:51–111) and of the *Alma Redemptoris Mater* (92:262), are otherwise exact.

## Second research pass

The first collation rowed 29 statements of the text as unverified leads. Before any of them was narrowed or removed, a second research pass went to the source families each claim belongs to, and the coordinator's later rewording of nine passages was read again against the records. The families and loci pursued, by claim:

- **The present Liturgy of the Hours** (05:21, 05:39, 10:36, 22:18, 22:23, 36:11, 44:4, 92:274): the Council, *Sacrosanctum Concilium* n. 89 (Latin, AAS 56, p. 122); the promulgating constitution, *Laudis canticum* n. 2 (AAS 63, pp. 529–530); the General Instruction (*Institutio generalis de Liturgia Horarum*), nn. 37–38, 50, 55–59, 84–91, 138 and 237–239; and the AELF daily Office, Laudes and Complies of 1 December 2026, for the Gospel canticles in use. The Latin typical text of the General Instruction was sought and no public text of it was found. That is evidence unavailable, not a reading that found the claim absent: the English of 1974 (Australia, England and Wales, Ireland; restricted) serves in its place, paraphrased with its numbers and never quoted.
- **The appointments of the Mass** (26:42, 31:142, 36:30, 90:53, 90:343, 90:369, 90:375): the USCCB *Liturgical Calendar* for 2026, entries of 8 November (printed p. 43), 12 December (p. 46) and 25 December (p. 47); the rubric of the Vigil Mass of the Nativity in the Missal of 2002; and the tracked Clementine Vulgate and Douay verse texts of 1 Thessalonians 4.
- **Saints and authors** (12:3, 14:61, 24:85, 30:55, 30:111, 31:53, 31:79 with 31:93, 32:92, 36:64): the Roman Martyrology of 1902 (18 March, 14 and 16 September, 24 November, 3 and 14 December); Gifford's life of St Cyril in the Nicene and Post-Nicene Fathers, 2nd series, vol. 7; Coleridge's *Life and Letters of St Francis Xavier*, vol. I, pp. 287–288; the front matter of Migne's *Patrologia Latina* vols. 194 and 105; the head-title of Durandus's *Rationale* in the edition of 1568, with Guéranger's *Liturgical Year*, vol. I, p. 26, for his century; M. B. Riddle's introductory essay to Augustine's *Harmony of the Gospels*; and John Paul II's homily of 3 September 2000.
- **The older Office** (14:86): the general rubrics of the Breviary of 1888, title XXXI, on the *Te Deum*.
- **Wording changed after the first pass** (10:46, 10:78, 14:63, 20:52, 30:100, 31:80, 32:39–41, 32:93–95, 92:156): the records already held, namely the AELF responsory of the First Sunday, the Catena on Matthew 25:9 and Mark 13:37, Amalarius III.40, Chrysostom's Homily 19 on John, the work record of *Ineffabilis Deus*, Augustine's Sermon 293.3 (PL 38, cols. 1328–1329) with John 1:20, the Lewis romances (pp. 426–432) and Guéranger, p. 155.

What the pass changed:

- No row now rests on a lead. Of the 29, 14 rows are now verified source text, 8 checked quotation or paraphrase and 1 source-grounded synthesis (26:42, the Lectionary's verse numbering, computed from the 2026 calendar and the Vulgate). The other 6 rows were rewritten to follow the reworded text: 20:52 (checked); 31:79 and 31:80 (verified, with a new row for the credit at 31:93); 31:142 (checked); 32:39–41 (two verified rows and one synthesis); 32:93 (verified); 92:156 (verified). The witnesses for 12:3, 30:55, 30:111 (Japan), 36:30 and the 2026 calendar rows are inspected but not verified, so those rows stay at checked.
- The wording differences the first pass reported (10:45–46, 10:76–77, 14:63, 30:100, 31:79) are gone: the text now says what its records say, and those rows have been re-keyed to their present lines.
- With the General Instruction n. 50 in hand, three rows on the Gospel canticles of Evening Prayer (05:21, 22:20, 92:294) rise to verified, and 42:63 gains it as a second witness.
- Four rows of Appendix B (92:327, 92:338, 92:348, 92:349) were moved to the lines that hold their claims.

Thinner support, rowed as synthesis and worth a second look: 05:22 (Night Prayer "before sleep", from Bute's note on Compline); 14:47 ("later ages called the Ember days"); 40:5 (the name Bethlehem "in the Hebrew"); 40:49 (Bernard "to his monks"); 40:56 (Greccio "in the hills of central Italy"; the letter says "near Rieti"); 40:77 (Francis "urged" a crib at home; n. 3 says it "helps"); 44:18 (the letter's "unknown author"; ANF heads it "Mathetes"); 44:65 (Preface III "through the Christmas season"; the record gives the title only); 48:16 (Leo "to the people of Rome"); 48:31 (Thérèse "with her family"); 90:343 (the Guadalupe Office of Readings "of the feast", with the Te Deum, from the U.S. rank and the calendar's proper psalter).
